# DEBT — closed rows, moved by the archiver

**Written only by `node tools/ledger.mjs archive <ID>`**, which moves a closed row with no
declared residue (the one definition is `isClosedDebtRow` in `tools/owed.mjs`) out of
`docs/development/DEBT.md` VERBATIM and appends it here, and refuses the move unless every id
occurs exactly as often in the live ledger plus its archive after the move as before. Rows
arrive in the order they were archived. Find any id with `node tools/ledger.mjs find <ID>`.
The August roll (`DEBT-closed-2026-08.md`) is a separate closed file and is never appended to.

| ID | Sev | Found | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| D-282 | defect | 2026-08-09 | **A SUITE THAT FAILS WITH A LARGE ENOUGH DUMP LOSES ITS OWN TALLY UNDER THE BATTERY, BECAUSE `process.exit()` DISCARDS UNFLUSHED PIPE WRITES — D-93's SHAPE THROUGH A MECHANISM D-93 NEVER NAMED.** Found 2026-08-09 by D-249's control arm catching ITSELF: the `widen` arm reported a tally of `-1` and a verdict of NOT AS DECLARED, and **the arm was right while the HARNESS was wrong**. `hygiene.test.mjs` ends `process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0)`, as `hygiene`'s own dispose rule requires. When stdout is a **PIPE** those writes are ASYNCHRONOUS and `process.exit` returns to the OS without flushing them. **MEASURED ON ONE ARMED TREE, SAME RUN, TWO CAPTURES: 191,434 bytes reached a FILE and 89,329 bytes reached a PIPE — 102,105 bytes of TAIL discarded, and the tail is where the tally lives.** **IT IS NOT `maxBuffer`: raising it to 64 MB changed nothing**, because the bytes were never written; that hypothesis was tested and killed rather than assumed. **THIS IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THAT ONE CONTROL. `scripts/battery.mjs:413` spawns every suite with DEFAULT stdio, which is a pipe**, so any suite failing with a dump past roughly the pipe buffer loses its tally under the battery while the early lines survive — the reader sees `FAILED:` lines and no count, or a truncated count, and D-93 exists precisely because a suite that reports no tally reads as a suite that was never run. **THE AMPLIFIER IS `t()` ITSELF:** the failure LABEL is capped at 8 entries by D-237's cap, but `t()` then prints `want … got …` with the **FULL** array, so a check that floods produces a single 146,820-character line and the cap defends nothing at the only moment it matters. **WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED:** the exact threshold was not bisected — 89 KB survived and 191 KB did not, on one machine, with one pipe buffer — and no historical battery failure has been re-examined for a truncated tail, so how often this has already happened is UNKNOWN and is stated as unknown. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: either cap `t()`'s `got` dump the way its label is already capped (the smaller fix, and it makes every large failure readable rather than merely survivable), or have suites flush before exiting; plus an assertion that a deliberately-flooding suite still reports its tally through a pipe. Relation: D-93 (the tally that never arrives), D-237 (the cap this defeats). | M0 · **CLOSED 2026-08-10 (D-282). THE FIX IS THE SECOND OF THE TWO THIS ROW OFFERED — SUITES FLUSH BEFORE EXITING — AND THE CAP WAS REJECTED ON A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN ON TASTE.** `bio-plane/test/stdio.mjs` (NEW) makes stdout and stderr SYNCHRONOUS before a suite writes anything, so `process.exit` has nothing queued to discard. It is imported for its SIDE EFFECT by all 153 `.test.mjs` suites — one added line each and nothing else moved in any of them — and by `sandbox.mjs`, so the control and probe harnesses that already take that module's side effect take this one too; **D-282 was found by a CONTROL ARM and not by the battery, so a fix that reached only the battery would have left the discovery path open.** `hygiene.test.mjs` gains the census that makes a 154th suite without it fail the run. **WHY NOT THE CAP, in four measured grounds: (1) IT MOVES THE THRESHOLD RATHER THAN REMOVING IT** — with every line ALREADY capped at 512 bytes the tally still went missing 1 run in 5 at ~1,563 capped failures and stayed lost at 3,125 and 6,250; **(2) IT LEAVES A RACE** — three identical runs at 1,024-byte writes and 2,000,000 bytes total came back MISSING, MISSING, PRESENT, and a fix that is right most of the time in the instrument every other item is judged by is not a fix; **(3) IT DEFENDS ONE WRITER** — `t()` is copy-pasted into every suite, so the cap is 150-odd edits that still say nothing about a stack trace or any `console.log` that is not `t()`; **(4) IT IS THE OPPOSITE DEFECT ONE STEP AWAY, AND THE CONTROL ARM SHOWS IT EXACTLY.** Armed with the cap, **every arm that measures LOSS goes GREEN** — the tally arrives, and the pipe and file captures agree perfectly at 312 bytes each — **while the MIDDLE and the TAIL of a perfectly readable 1,000,156-byte diagnosis are destroyed.** The cap is the fix that would have looked green. **NOTHING IS CAPPED HERE, AND THAT IS A CHOICE RATHER THAN AN OMISSION:** with the flush in place the whole dump ARRIVES — 1,000,156 bytes through a pipe, byte-identical to the same child's file capture — and readability of a 146,820-character line is a real but SEPARATE and much smaller question than loss. **THE THRESHOLD THIS ROW NAMED UNDETERMINED IS NOW BISECTED, AND THE ROW'S MODEL OF IT WAS TOO SIMPLE — CORRECTED HERE.** For a SINGLE write it is the pipe buffer exactly: 65,573 bytes survive and 65,580 do not, and every partial arrival observed is an exact multiple of **65,536**. But it is **not one number**, because there are two independent variables: 187,516 bytes delivered in 470-byte writes survived intact, while 2,000,000 bytes delivered in 1,024-byte writes did not — a child racing a parent that is draining, which is why the loss is nondeterministic at the margin. **AND IT IS PLATFORM-SPECIFIC, WHICH THE ROW NEVER SAID:** node writes to a pipe SYNCHRONOUSLY on Linux and Windows and ASYNCHRONOUSLY on macOS, so this defect cannot occur on a Linux CI machine at all — part of why it survived as long as it did, and the reason `tally-through-pipe.test.mjs` runs the UNFIXED fixture as a reach arm and reports NOT-EXHIBITED by name rather than passing quietly on a platform where arm A could not fail. **THE HISTORICAL REACH IS NARROWED AND STILL NOT ESTABLISHED, AND IT STAYS STATED AS SUCH.** No previously reported battery figure in this corpus is SHOWN to have been truncated: every `assertions unknown` record in `MEASUREMENTS.md` and `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md` is attributed at its site to a suite that printed no tally AT ALL — `bundle.test.mjs` and `livefire.test.mjs` by construction, `airun.test.mjs`'s ReferenceError, and one suite whose tally read `N pass, M FAIL` against a lower-case matcher — and not to a discarded tail; and the runner SELF-REPORTS every suite whose count it could not read, so a truncation could not have been silent in any run whose summary was quoted. What makes that structural rather than lucky is measured: **the largest GREEN suite output in this battery is `hygiene.test.mjs` at 46,399 bytes, 71% of one pipe buffer**, and green output is thousands of small writes against an actively draining reader, which survives far past 64 KiB. **THE EXPOSURE WAS ALWAYS THE FAILING RUN**, and the one recorded instance is D-249's arm, already in this row. **WHAT REMAINS UNKNOWN AND IS NOT ROUNDED UP: no raw output of any historical failing run was kept, so none can be re-examined.** NEGATIVE CONTROL: `bio-plane/test/tally-through-pipe.control.mjs`, four arms, **4 of 4 AS DECLARED, 0 restore failures**, each armed alone, every restore verified by sha256 AND content against a uniquely-named pristine copy with a 1,000-byte floor. **TWO OF THE FOUR DECLARATIONS CAME BACK NOT AS DECLARED ON THE FIRST RUN AND BOTH TIMES THE ARM WAS RIGHT AND THE DECLARATION WAS WRONG** — the inverse of the way this row was found — and both are corrected and recorded rather than quietly rewritten: `unflush` was declared to fail all four intactness assertions when the HEAD of the diagnosis must SURVIVE (the early lines live, the tail dies, which is the defect's own signature), and `overstrict` was declared to fail byte-equality when capping changes what the child SAYS and not what the pipe delivers. `hygiene` STAYS GREEN under `unflush`, which is a finding and not a comfort: the census is a SPELLING check and cannot see a neutered module, so `tally-through-pipe.test.mjs` is the load-bearing half. **ON `CLAUDE.md`'s RULE ABOUT A COMMAND PIPED INTO `tail` REPORTING TAIL's STATUS, ASKED AND ANSWERED EITHER WAY:** `battery.mjs` reads each child's status from `child.on("close", code)` — the kernel wait status directly — and contains no shell pipeline, no `shell: true` and no `execSync`, so that hazard does not reach the runner's own reading. It reaches the CALLER who pipes `npm run test:battery` into `tail`, which is the false `exit 0` recorded 2026-08-04, and it is unchanged by this item. Battery 158/158 green; `civicos-ui/test/**` carries the same exposure, is not this session's to edit, and is DELEGATED in `CLAIMS.md`. **CLOSED ON THE SECOND ESTATE 2026-09-16 (M0-36), WHICH IS WHERE THE DELEGATION ABOVE FINALLY DRAINED — five weeks, and nothing drains a delegation on a schedule.** All **53** writers in `civicos-ui/test/` that call `process.exit` now import the same module: the **41** `.test.mjs` suites `run.mjs` discovers and spawns piped, the **11** `.control.mjs`/harness drivers beside them, and **`run.mjs` itself**, which prints `civicos-ui: all harnesses green` after a `process.exit(1)` branch. **THE MODULE IS SHARED ACROSS THE PACKAGE BOUNDARY RATHER THAN COPIED, and the reason is this row's own mechanism turned on the fix:** the module reaches the PRIVATE `_handle.setBlocking`, and `tally-through-pipe.test.mjs` is what turns the day node closes that door into a red battery — a second copy in the UI estate would be a second unguarded use of a private API, which is the failure mode being guarded against, reintroduced by the fix for it. `run.mjs` already imports `../../bio-plane/scripts/provenance.mjs`, so the hop was the estate's convention before it was this item's choice. **THE POPULATION WAS RE-MEASURED AND BOTH OF THE QUEUE ROW'S FIGURES WERE RIGHT ABOUT DIFFERENT POPULATIONS** — `53` counted the whole directory's unguarded writers and `38` counted only `.test.mjs` files containing no occurrence of the string `stdio` at all, under-counting the suites by three (`auth-surface`, `intent-write`, `refusal-codes`) which spawn piped children of their own and so matched a scan for the substring while importing nothing. Neither was the number to edit on, and the row said so. **THE HARNESSES ARE IN SCOPE BECAUSE THIS ROW WAS FOUND BY A CONTROL ARM CATCHING ITSELF** reporting a tally of `-1`: a control driver that loses its own verdict through a pipe produces a confident wrong refutation, which is the most expensive output this estate has. Standing census: `civicos-ui/test/stdio-census.test.mjs`, 14 assertions, which asserts the POPULATION rather than a count in a row, DRIVES the module through a real pipe, tests its own matcher in both directions, and pins the residual. **THE NEW CENSUS WAS ITSELF CAUGHT BY THE ESTATE AT THIS ITEM'S OWN GATE AND THE CATCH WAS RIGHT:** `hygiene.test.mjs`'s M0-16/D-301 arm failed it as an unguarded directory walk — a file that discovers its own corpus and prints a population is the shape that let M0-15's phantom into a baseline — so it is GUARDED by `scripts/provenance.mjs` rather than added to hygiene's named-and-unguarded list, and it now prints `67 of 69 discovered item(s) are in the commit at HEAD` beside its own figures. A census whose headline number a reader will quote owes the reproducible total beside the contaminated one. NEGATIVE CONTROL: `civicos-ui/test/nc-m036.mjs`, **7 of 7 arms as declared**, every one driven through a PIPE and none through a terminal. **THE RATE, 40 samples per cell: without the import the tally is lost 30/40 at ~410 KB and 39/40 at ~2.4 MB; with it, 0/80, and the delivered byte count is IDENTICAL AND COMPLETE in every sample.** The minimum arrival is 65,581 bytes — the 64 KiB pipe buffer, the signature `stdio.mjs`'s header records, reproduced independently here. **TWO DECLARATIONS CAME BACK WRONG BEFORE THE ARM DID AND BOTH TIMES THE ARM WAS RIGHT:** declared *lost in every one of 3 runs* it measured 1 of 3, and re-declared *lost at least once in 3* it measured 0 of 3 on a quieter machine. The loss is a RACE whose rate moves with machine load, so the correction was a change of KIND rather than a better guess — stop declaring an OUTCOME, count SAMPLES, declare the comparison of rates — and the through-the-real-runner cell now carries NO verdict at all, because three samples cannot decide a coin and an arm that declares one produces a confident wrong answer in whichever direction the machine was leaning. **THE RESIDUAL IS NAMED RATHER THAN CLOSED: the three `civicos-ui/check-*.mjs` guards are outside M0-36's region and still exit unflushed** — `check-semantics.mjs` has the live form, printing its `OK:` verdict on the line before `process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0)` — DELEGATED to UI in `CLAIMS.md` and printed by the census's ARM D every run so the gap cannot go quiet. **AND THIS ROW'S "IT IS NOT `maxBuffer`" WAS TRUE OF `battery.mjs` AND NOT OF THE UI RUNNER — see D-387**, which M0-36's own control arm found by coming back RED over a working subject. |

CPDF-16's one un-mechanised measurement is mechanised beside it: each pinned expression, and each anchor the guards `grab` through, must occur EXACTLY ONCE (`String.replace` rewrites the first occurrence only, so a duplicate disarms every mutation arm silently). **THE DEFECT WAS REPRODUCED BEFORE IT WAS FIXED, against the guards as committed at `cebf564` extracted into a scratch tree: 10 arms, 7 NOT AS DECLARED — all four superstring arms left BOTH guards at exit 0, and D-315's own arm (`* 100` → `* 100.0`) reproduced exactly.** The same driver against the fixed guards: **10 arms, 0 not as declared**, exit 0. **THE SWEEP WIDENED THE ROW, and this is the part D-315 did not name: `norm()` and `levenshteinPairs()` are not merely READ out of the floor — both probes IMPORT AND RUN them to compute every number they print — and they carried no identity check at all** (`norm` only indirectly, through PIN_GT, and only for mutations that change `norm(GT_PAGE2)`; `levenshteinPairs` in no direction whatever). Both are now digest-pinned, and both arms passed silently pre-fix. Driver: `bio-plane/test/d315-guard-controls.mjs`, every arm ALONE against the REAL floor, restores verified by sha256 AND `cmp` against uniquely-named per-arm pristine copies with the byte count printed and floored, and a foot that re-checks the floor against its pre-run digest. | D-109 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **Nothing drains the task queue on a schedule, so an auto-created task appears only when somebody calls `op=taskdrain`.** D-98 shipped the producer, the consumer and the split; what it did not ship is a trigger. The RULED behaviour is that an undetermined capture creates a task AUTOMATICALLY at capture, and it now does exactly that up to the queue boundary, but the task itself waits for a drain that nothing issues. Draining from the capture path was considered and rejected: it would put the daemon credential back in the position of causing the inbox write, which is the coupling the producer/consumer split exists to break, even though the consumer would still be deciding every field. The natural home is a Durable Object alarm, the mechanism `selections` already uses (`#armSweep`, self-terminating when the table empties), armed on enqueue and re-armed while `task_queue` is non-empty. That keeps the trigger inside the DO and inside the audit model, and it costs an idle instance no timer. Until it exists the inbox is correct but not yet automatic, and the word 'automatically' in the ruling is not fully honoured. **CLOSED 2026-09-14 — discharged in fact, found by CAP-6's retrofit measurement and closed by CONDUCT at its integration: a `task-drain` consumer is registered on the reconciling DO alarm (`store.mjs` ~:1987, `TASK_DRAIN_ALARM_BATCH = 200`), which is exactly the home this row proposed; the word "automatically" in the ruling is honoured.** | M1 · closed 2026-09-14 |
| D-196 | design | 2026-08-04 | **The completeness statement is PROSE with nothing behind it, which the search-completeness research identifies as the exact claim the field considers worthless.** MEASURED: `completenessFields` (bio-checks.mjs) carries `statement` (authored prose about what the case does NOT cover), `subject_justification`, and `excluded` rows of (target, description, reason). **It records NOTHING about what was SEARCHED**, and `excluded` can only hold DELIBERATE omissions — things someone knew of and left out — so there is no representation at all for what nobody knew existed. The literature's verdict on unbacked completeness claims is Blair & Maron (1985): attorneys stipulated they must reach 75% recall and sincerely believed they had; measured recall was ~20%, after iteration and refinement. **THE FIX IS ALREADY DESIGNED UNDER ANOTHER NAME.** `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` argues for an append-only OBSERVATION LOG — every look, including the ones returning nothing — on provenance grounds. That is also, exactly, the disclosed-process half of a defensible completeness claim: *we searched these sources, at these levels, on these dates, on these leads, and this came back, including the empty results*. That is Rule 26(g)'s *reasonable inquiry* made checkable. **The two designs are ONE design** and neither works alone: an observation log nobody publishes is bookkeeping, and a completeness statement with nothing behind it is Blair & Maron. What must NOT be built: a recall-like number (the denominator is unknowable at BIO's prevalence and Goodhart is invoked by name in the literature) or elusion testing (its inventors have asked the profession to discontinue it; it overstated recall by 16-36 points in live litigation, and its failure mode is assessing one's own work). See `research/SEARCH-COMPLETENESS.md`. **DESIGNED 2026-09-14 (BOB #11): `docs/development/OBSERVATION-LOG-DESIGN.md` §6 — the completeness statement's `searched` section computed from the observation log at case signing; item 4 of that design's decomposition.** | M10 - **CLOSED 2026-09-17 by REC-96** · the completeness statement now carries a `searched` section computed from the observation log at case signing over **the case's own subjects** — its members' basis legs, the `content` rows those legs name and the captures under them — published in the SIGNED bytes in both frontmatter and body, and refused by C-41.10 when absent. **WHAT CLOSES THE ROW IS THE SUBJECT SET AND NOT THE SECTION**, because the section alone is the defect this row names wearing a new shape: computed over the observation log's own subjects it would read 100% searched BY CONSTRUCTION with every row in it honest and every number true, which is Blair & Maron exactly — what was measured not being what was claimed. So `subject_source` is a member of a vocabulary the log is deliberately NOT in (`SEARCHED_SUBJECT_SOURCES`, `bio-checks.mjs`), the gate checks it on bytes a stranger hands us, and the lie is unrepresentable rather than discouraged; the control arm that swaps the set forges nothing, keeps every state true, and turns a never-looked case into a SEARCHED one. **NO RECALL FIGURE IS PUBLISHED and none is computable** — the row's own prohibition, honoured: the denominator is unknowable and the section says so in prose. Three honest negatives are first-class and PUBLISH: `never_looked` where §5.1's cause (3) is established, `undetermined` where the pre-log or purge cause cannot be excluded, and `no_subjects` where the question was not askable — the over-strictness arm proves a gate refusing them makes the honest case unpublishable and pressures a member into inventing coverage. Driven end to end in `bio-plane/test/casesearched.test.mjs` (21 assertions, 4 control arms + baseline). |
| D-186 | defect | 2026-08-04 | **The test battery leaks one OS temp directory per miniflare instance and never cleans up, and it filled the development machine's disk to ZERO.** MEASURED 2026-08-04 when a planning-surface write failed with ENOSPC: **23,263 `miniflare-*` directories holding 41.0 GB** in the user's `$TMPDIR`, plus ~2,300 more from the suites' own sandboxes (`ratify-`, `sshsig-`, `signpage-`, `attest-`, `reuse-ratify-`, `publish-`, `publishedcase-`, `reeval-`) and 595 MB from the OCR probes (`cpdf9-ocr-`, `cpdf11-moondream-`, which npm-install engines into temp by design). 36 of 42 suites drive the plane through miniflare, CONDUCT runs the FULL battery on every worker landing, and nothing removes the sandbox afterward — so the leak scales with integration throughput and accumulated ~23k directories in days. **THE COST IS NOT DISK, IT IS THE THING THAT STOPPED:** at zero bytes every Bash command failed before it started (the harness could not write its own output file), so the session could not commit, push, or reach CONDUCT at all — a planning surface that is written and unpublishable is exactly the failure `plancheck` exists to prevent, arriving by a route it cannot see. Reclaimed 37.2 GB by deleting only dirs older than the running battery (a worker was mid-`test:battery`; deleting its live sandbox would have broken CONDUCT's verification — the fix must be equally careful). NEEDS: the harness disposes of its miniflare sandbox in a `finally`, the suites dispose of their own, and the battery sweeps ORPHANS older than the current run at start; the OCR probes' npm installs are separately bounded. A negative control exists for free — run the battery twice and assert the temp-dir count does not grow. | M0 · CLOSED 2026-08-04 by M0-8, and M0-8 CORRECTED ITS MECHANISM — record the correction, because the intuition in the row above is backwards and the next reader will have it too. **The leak is the SUCCESS path, not the failure path.** `Miniflare#dispose()` (4.20260722.0, `dist/src/index.js:104655`) takes down its own synchronous `process.on("exit")` cleanup and THEN starts removing the sandbox without awaiting it; every suite ends `await mf.dispose(); process.exit(…)` — which `hygiene.test.mjs` REQUIRES, so a lingering handle cannot turn a green run into a hang — and the exit beats the unawaited removal with the safety net already gone. Measured three modes × three runs: succeed-and-exit leaked 3/3, THROW leaked 0/3 (miniflare's exit hook is still armed when a suite never reaches dispose), dispose+250 ms settle leaked 0/3. So a `finally` would have fixed nothing, and 23,263 directories in days is the record of a battery **working**. FIX: `bio-plane/test/sandbox.mjs` owns `$TMPDIR` for the process and `rmSync`s it synchronously on exit and on signal — which covers the suites' own `ratify-`/`sshsig-`/`attest-`/… sandboxes for free, since they all `mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),…))`; `hygiene.test.mjs` requires the import of every suite that builds a Miniflare or mkdtemps, so the list cannot fall behind the directory (D-93's lesson); `scripts/battery.mjs` sweeps orphans by OWNER pid where the name carries one and by age-with-2h-grace where it does not, and spares outright anything a live `workerd`/`node` holds open (`lsof`) — which is not decorative: a foreign worktree's suite was found holding a sandbox after 1h28m while this was being written. The battery now ASSERTS it, from a temp directory of its own that it hands to every child, so the count is attributable and a concurrent un-fixed checkout cannot redden the run. Verified: battery green twice, 95/95, 5172 assertions, 0 residue each; NC (remove the exit disposal) → 84 sandboxes leaked and the run fails while all 95 suites still report green. RESIDUAL, deliberately left: the OCR/PDF probes are out of scope by the item, and `tier1-coverage-probe.mjs`'s persistent `$TMPDIR` cache is why they must stay out. **CORRECTED 2026-08-08 by M0-10 (D-235), and the correction is about the INSTRUMENT rather than the fix: the hygiene assertion this row installed certified that a suite IMPORTS `sandbox.mjs`, never that its files LAND inside the `$TMPDIR` the import redirects.** Two suites imported it and wrote to hardcoded `/tmp` paths anyway, so both passed while leaking outside the fence — and because `battery.mjs` counts residue only INSIDE `$TMPDIR`, the leak was not under-reported but invisible (`0 directories, 0 sandboxes` printed over 12 MB accumulating in `/tmp/mfp` since 2026-07-31). The containment half is now asserted; see D-235. **THE FENCE ITSELF IS SOUND AND IS NOW MEASURED UNDER GENUINE CONCURRENCY** — the question M0-13 explicitly could not settle: 12 truly overlapping processes, distinct sandbox each, no cross-contamination, 0 left after exit, with an ARMED probe that reports DOES NOT HOLD when the sandbox is shared. *(Disposition head reworded by CONDUCT #5, 2026-09-18, for LED-3: the row was closed in fact but its disposition did not LEAD with a closure word, so the one CLOSED definition in `tools/owed.mjs` read it as open. Only the head changed; the substance is as written.)* |
| D-183 | design | 2026-08-04 | **A derived transcription's GRADE rests on a measurement, and nothing binds the transcription to the measurement that justified it — so an engine that changes silently invalidates grades with no way to find which ones.** Named by Bob 2026-08-04: *"given that web services are occasionally upgraded, and a Moondream revision could affect the trust level."* The chain records WHO derived the text (`ocr(engine, version)`, DEC-4) and CPDF-11 sets the engine's fidelity CAP by measurement — but the cap is a fact about the engine AT A DATE, and the transcription records no reference to it. So when a re-probe returns a different number, the blast radius is unknowable: every transcription ever made by that engine is equally suspect and equally unidentifiable. **THE FIX IS ONE FIELD AND ONE CONSTRUCT: a CALIBRATION** — a dated, identified measurement of a derivation engine's fidelity, which the chain REFERENCES (`ocr(moondream3.1, calibration: CAL-n)`), so a superseded calibration names exactly the transcriptions resting on it. **AND THE DRIFT HANDLER IS ASYMMETRIC, which is the part a build session will not derive**: a calibration that measures WORSE means legs resting on the old cap now overclaim, so it SURFACES a re-evaluation obligation (REC-17's shape, DEC-16's event propagation) and recomputes nothing on the member's behalf; a calibration that measures BETTER raises NOTHING automatically, because a grade rises only by an authored act (DEC-4, no machine mints a grade). Silent re-grading in either direction is the defect. **THIS GENERALISES BEYOND OCR and is the reason to build it once**: Tier-2 pdf.js has a pinned version that will move, an AI post-processor is a second derivation, an external service retrains under an unchanged name (DEC-35's own argument against Textract) — three engines with one problem, the D-164 lesson applied before rather than after. Trigger for the re-probe is the REC-1 alarm (a sixth consumer); a vendor CHANGELOG watch may only ACCELERATE a probe and may never satisfy one, because absence of an announcement is not evidence of no change and a vendor's documentation is a claim, not a measurement. | M2 · **CLOSED 2026-09-10 by CPDF-13** (worker `agent-ab1e3df0de5e3b8ec`, branch `worktree-agent-ab1e3df0de5e3b8ec`) — the construct is `bio-plane/src/calibration.mjs`, the storage is I5's three new tables (IC-72), the chain reference is I2's one optional field (IC-73), and the clock is the ELEVENTH `#schedConsumers` entry (`calibration-reprobe`, cadence `CALIBRATION_CADENCE_MS` = 30 days, declared in one place and quoted by name in SCHEDULER.md). **THE ASYMMETRY IS BUILT AND DRIVEN**: a WORSE calibration raises a re-evaluation obligation naming exactly the transcriptions bound to the superseded measurement and re-grades NOTHING (REC-17's derived shape — the obligation is a QUERY and is stored nowhere); a BETTER one raises nothing at all. Both of the item's negative controls RUN and recorded in `test/calibration.test.mjs`'s `NEGATIVE CONTROL:` line, and **both had to be armed by ADDING code rather than removing it, because the handler writes nothing there is a guard to delete**. NOTE ON THE SEQUENCING THIS ROW PREDICTED: CPDF-11 returned NO-GO on Moondream (DEC-42), so the engine that provoked this row is not the engine that will first use it — Tier-2 pdf.js is, exactly as the row's own generalisation argument said it would be. **WHAT IS NOT CLOSED AND IS NOT THIS ROW'S:** nothing in this repository RUNS a fidelity probe, because no derivation engine is deployed here; the consumer marks a subject OWED and says so rather than treating the last measurement as current. |

Two live consequences, neither of them theoretical: (1) **REC-26's `SELF` binding never took effect.** The delegation landed it in `wrangler.jsonc` and stated its acceptance as deploy-gated; the deploy then happened (0.56.0, 2026-08-04) and did not carry it, so CAP-3's archive-monitor and REC-26's monitor-cadence are STILL built-and-wired-to-nothing, exactly as `MACHINE-PROCESSES.md` §0 measured — and the landing looks complete in every document. (2) **`PDF_WORKER` is unbound, so `op=pdfstructure` degrades to Tier 1 on the live plane** (D-115's un-fleeted path) — and `pdf-worker` is not deployed to the account at all (**HTTP 404**), so the binding could not have resolved even if sent. PARTIAL FIX APPLIED 2026-08-05 (DIST): `service` added to `keep_bindings`, which is the PROTECTIVE half — it stops a future deploy deleting a service binding that is present, and changes nothing today because none is. **NOT FIXED, and deliberately not by DIST mid-cut:** actually SENDING the declared bindings. That is a behavioural change — it ARMS the two monitoring consumers on a live instance, whose semantics belong to RECORD and CAPTURE, and it cannot work for `PDF_WORKER` until `pdf-worker` is deployed at all. NEEDS: `deploy.mjs` to derive its bindings FROM `wrangler.jsonc` rather than restate them, so the declared config is the deployed config by construction and this class cannot recur; plus a decision on deploying `pdf-worker`. | M1 · **CLOSED 2026-09-14 (DIST #2, `01e6b90`)** — the derivation half landed: `scripts/derive-bindings.mjs` derives the deployed bindings from `wrangler.jsonc` with the slug substituted (D-292), refuses any config class it does not carry, and `deploy.mjs` pre-flights every derived service target. The ARMING half is declared, not yet fired: the next deploy through the script sends SELF and the fleet bindings — RECORD and CAPTURE told by delegation (CLAIMS.md 2026-09-14) before any tick fires, and biosmoke7's first armed tick spends the scoped daemon credential (DIST-4), not the root of trust |

So the variance is a LOUD skip, not silence, and the standing discipline — measure your own baseline — handled all three sightings correctly. | D-283 | gap | 2026-08-09 | **THE TIER-2 ESCALATION REPLACES A DOCUMENT'S TEXT WHOLESALE TOO, AND IT IS THE SAME CLASS AS D-252 WITH A DIFFERENT RULE.** Found by D-252's class sweep, which asked in principle where the plane takes a DOCUMENT-level decision about a per-PAGE fact and then assigns a producer's whole answer over it. What is unbounded is TEXT LOSS: the predicate is a document-level ratio (`undetermined > chars`), so a document where Tier 1 decoded page 0 well and failed page 1 escalates whole, and if Tier 2 recovers page 1 but does worse on page 0 the plane keeps the worse page silently. NOT MEASURED, and that is stated rather than guessed — measuring it needs pdf.js against a corpus of partially-decodable PDFs, which no fixture in this repository has. NEEDS: a per-page rule for which of two decodes of the same layer wins, which is a real question (character count is exactly the instrument CPDF-9 argued against) and is a different item from D-252. An installable fleet therefore REQUIRES one bundled, hashed, signed artifact per member — the very build step FLEET deferred. **I BUILT THAT BUILD STEP AND THEN BACKED IT OUT, deliberately**: copying `pdf-worker/scripts/build.mjs` into `agent-worker` works (measured: 48,392 bytes, `node --check` clean, its own 113 assertions green against source), but shipping it would have silently reversed another area's recorded decision inside a DIST commit. | D-305 | defect | 2026-09-10 | **THE MINTED-DIGIT METRIC IS BLIND TO A DIGIT SUBSTITUTION, AND IT READ CLEAN OVER THE WORST TRANSCRIPTION CPDF-14 MEASURED.** The floor's expression (CPDF-9, `ocr-measure-probe.mjs`) counts a digit as MINTED when the output has a digit with **no digit opposite it** in the ground truth: `pairs.filter(([g, o]) => o && /[0-9]/.test(o) && (!g \|\| !/[0-9]/.test(g))).length`. A digit swapped for another digit is aligned digit-to-digit, so it is scored as a digit ERROR and mints nothing. MEASURED 2026-09-10, CPDF-14's run of record, ladder rung R3 under the composed shape: **MINTED 0 while 13 of 17 ground-truth digits were WRONG** — the model returned *"any one transportation which amounts $10,000"* against a truth of *"any one transaction which exceeds $50,000"*. **A reader watching the MINTED column would have called that rung clean**; only the GT-digits column (4/17) saw it. This matters beyond one probe: "minted digits" is the headline number CPDF-11's NO-GO rests on, it is the number a CPDF-13 calibration would store for any future engine, and the money figure a case turns on is exactly the thing a substitution destroys silently. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: a second expression beside the existing one — digit-position DISAGREEMENT, counted and reported as its own column — added to the floor instrument, which is CPDF-9's file and therefore a comparability-bearing edit: every probe reading that file pins its four expressions and one pins the ground truth by digest, so the change must be ADDITIVE (a fifth expression) or every prior figure becomes incomparable. NOT closed here: CPDF-14 is a measurement item and moving the floor's own arithmetic mid-measurement is the thing its comparability guard exists to refuse. | ACCEPTED · CLOSED 2026-09-11 by CPDF-16 — the FIFTH EXPRESSION is in the floor (`ocr-measure-probe.mjs`), ADDITIVE beside the four pinned ones: digit-position DISAGREEMENT, a position where the ground truth has a digit, the output has a digit OPPOSITE it, and they differ — exactly the class `minted` is blind to. The D-305 exhibit is driven on EVERY run of the instrument before any engine is installed ($50,000 -> $10,000: disagree=1, minted=0, the run refuses to proceed if either side fails). Run of record 2026-09-11: all four committed columns reproduced EXACTLY (99.93%/99.96% chars, 90/90 digits, minted 1/0) with the new column reading 0 on the clean page — nothing moved, as the additive constraint predicted. Both landed probes' comparability guards exit 0 against the edited file and their own `--controls` suites came back all-as-declared (4/4, 5/5). NC (armed alone, `cpdf16-floor-controls.mjs`): a substitution-only corruption moved the new column to 1 and left minted at 0, BOTH asserted; a letter-for-letter error left the new column at 0 (over-strictness). |

| D-329 | defect | 2026-09-13 | **A CONTROL DRIVER CAN GO STALE BY QUOTING ITS OWN SUITE'S ASSERTION LABEL RATHER THAN ITS SUBJECT'S SOURCE, AND THAT SURFACE IS STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE TO ANY STATIC CHECK.** Found by M0-25's arm-liveness census. `aicredential.control.mjs` declared two arms against the fragment `every one of the 26 ops no member reaches is refused at the mint, by name`. The `mustNotFail` site (arm 1) went **SILENTLY VACUOUS**: a fragment that can never match can never be violated, so that guard has proved nothing and said nothing since the count moved. THE INSTANCE IS FIXED — both fragments now quote only the invariant part (`ops no member reaches is refused at the mint, by name`), which is the estate's "invert, do not lengthen a list" rule applied to a quotation. **THE ROW IS OPEN FOR THE CLASS.** M0-25's battery-side witness (`test/m025-arm-anchor-witness.test.mjs`) reads anchors that are LITERAL and looks for them in FILES; an assertion label composed at run time exists in no file, so no static instrument can see it and only a periodic census can. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: sweep every driver for a `mustFail`/`mustNotFail`/`expect` fragment quoting a label that carries a COMPOSED value, and make the rule explicit — a driver quotes the invariant part of an assertion name, never a rendered count. A list of key spellings would have gone stale at the first driver that did not use one, and that driver was the one the row was written about. THE RULE IS NOW ENFORCED RATHER THAN REMEMBERED: arm L3 IS the rule *a driver quotes the invariant part of an assertion name, never a rendered count*, and L4 drives it on this row's own historical instance — the 26-ops fragment is caught with `gap: "26"` while the hand repair that quotes only the invariant part passes. **TWO BLIND SPOTS STATED, NOT DISCOVERED LATER**: a label composed by `+` concatenation rather than by a template literal is invisible, and a rendered label that also sits verbatim somewhere in the code corpus is correctly scored present and stays silent. **AND ONE FINDING ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT, MEASURED**: the live corpus for a LABEL excludes `docs/`, because the RECORD quotes the defects it records — THIS ROW carries the stale fragment verbatim, and with `docs/` in, the check read this row and went green over a fully armed subject. The ANCHOR half keeps `docs/` in and must. | CLOSED 2026-09-14 |
| D-331 | design | 2026-09-13 | **A DEAD ANCHOR IS NOT A LOCAL FAILURE — IN HALF THIS ESTATE'S DRIVERS IT THROWS, AND EVERY ARM BEHIND IT GOES UNMEASURED AND UNREPORTED.** Measured by M0-25's census, and it is the most useful thing the run found about driver SHAPE rather than driver content. `casepin.control.mjs` had FOUR stale anchors; only ONE was visible, because `edit()` throws on a zero match and the throw happened at arm (a), so arms (c), (d) and (e) were never reached — their staleness was hidden behind the first one and the driver reported 2 of its 6 arm announcements. `casesign.control.mjs` and `caseproduction.control.mjs` behave the same way. The drivers that instead RECORD a zero match and continue (`harness.control.mjs`'s `arm()`, `walkfloor.control.mjs`, `analystvocab.control.mjs`) report every dead anchor in one run. **BOTH SHAPES ARE DEFENSIBLE and the difference was never a decision** — throwing keeps a half-armed tree from being measured, recording keeps the census complete — so this is a `design` row rather than a `defect`. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: rule on one shape for the estate and state the reason, OR require a throwing driver to VALIDATE EVERY ANCHOR BEFORE IT ARMS ANYTHING (a dry pass that counts each arm's quote and reports the whole set, then arms), which gets both properties and is the option this row recommends. | D-367 | defect | 2026-09-14 | **THE RULING INDEX'S MARKER MATCHES THE INDEX'S OWN FILENAME, SO PROSE THAT MERELY NAMES `DECIDED.md` MINTS A RULING ROW — ATTRIBUTED TO WHATEVER ID WAS LAST MENTIONED.** Found by M0-32's worker (2026-09-14) while reading the instrument rather than its output, and CONFIRMED at integration: `tools/decided.mjs`'s `MARKER` is `/\b(RULED|DECIDED|AMENDED|CORRECTED|OVERTURNED|SETTLED|SUPERSEDED|WITHDRAWN|CONCEDED)\b/` and **`.` IS A WORD BOUNDARY**, so the literal string `DECIDED.md` satisfies it. The filename occurs **48 times inside the generated index itself**, and the worker names at least eight rows already committed that are this artifact rather than rulings (D-293, D-311, IC-82, C-7.1, REC-85, UI-31, UI-58, UI-59). **WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN ITS SIZE:** `CLAUDE.md` tells every session to run `decided.mjs` BEFORE raising a question or writing a decision item, and the answer it returns is *the ruling in the words it was ruled in, plus the file and line that owns it*. A phantom row hands a session a ruling that was never made, attributed to an id that never made it — which is the overclaiming direction, inside the instrument this project uses to decide what is already settled. It is bounded rather than endemic: the index is a FLOOR by design and over-collection is deliberate (the file says so at the site), so a phantom row adds noise where a missing row would hide a ruling — the safer failure of the two, which is why nobody caught it for weeks. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** exclude a marker that is immediately followed by `.md` (or require the marker to be followed by a word boundary that is not a filename extension), regenerate, and **diff the old index against the new one rather than trusting the count** — the diff is the evidence about which rows were phantom, and it is a one-time measurement worth recording. The eight named rows are the control: they must disappear, and nothing else may. | M0 · **CLOSED 2026-09-15 by M0-34** (worker `agent-afc388ede4d357d33`, branch `worktree-agent-afc388ede4d357d33`), and the closure is a DIFF rather than a count. `MARKER` gains ONE clause — `(?!\.md\b)` — and nothing else in the tool moves. **THE DIFF, old index against new, adjudicated row by row against the SOURCE line each row points at: 951 rulings → 902. 49 pointers disappear and EVERY ONE of them is a filename match, 0 are not. All eight named rows are among them (D-293, D-311, IC-82, C-7.1, REC-85, UI-31, UI-58, UI-59) and the other 41 are the same artifact the row's own "at least eight" anticipated — THREE more carrying a namespace id (D-354, CAP-7, M0-29), 2 rendered under a date alone, and 36 carrying neither.** 0 new pointers appear, so nothing was unmasked by dedup and no real ruling moved either way. ONE pointer survives with a CORRECTED quote — `DEBT.md:304`, this row itself, which was quoted at the filename in its own headline and is now quoted at the `RULED` later in the row; two more (`QUEUE.md:714`, `BOB-NEXT.md:100`) re-anchor to a real marker and re-quote BYTE-IDENTICALLY. **The class is closed, not the instance:** `DECIDED.md` is the ONLY marker-plus-extension string anywhere in the scanned corpus, 110 of 110 occurrences, so no second filename is waiting. | D-368 | defect | 2026-09-15 | **THE REFERENCE-ITERATION CPU CURRENCY IS NOT RUNTIME-PORTABLE, AND EVERY FIGURE QUOTED IN IT COMPARES ACROSS RUNTIMES WITHOUT SAYING SO.** Measured by M0-31 (2026-09-14) on ONE machine in ONE minute: `burn()` reports **25,032 iterations/ms in node against 155,538 inside workerd — 6.21×**. The node half corroborates CONTENT-PDF's own 26,036 to within 4%, **so the disagreement is between RUNTIMES and not between dates or machines** — which is what makes it a defect in the currency rather than a stale number. WHAT IT COSTS WHILE OPEN: any figure expressed in reference iterations is comparable only to figures taken in the SAME runtime, and nothing in the record says which runtime a given figure was taken in — so a session comparing an OCR cost measured in node against a budget measured in workerd is out by 6× in the direction that makes an expensive thing look cheap. **M0-31 did NOT re-take or correct CONTENT-PDF's OCR row, and said so** — re-denominating another area's measurement from outside it is how a wrong number acquires a second author. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: an `op=cpuprobe` on a DEPLOYED Worker (not miniflare — M0-31's own workerd figure is miniflare-hosted and it says so), then either re-denominate every reference-iteration figure into a runtime-stamped unit or stamp each existing figure with the runtime it was taken in and forbid cross-runtime comparison at the site. The second is cheaper and is probably right: the figures are individually sound, and what is missing is the unit's context rather than the measurement. | M0 · **CLOSED 2026-09-16 by M0-35 — THE DEFECT IS REAL, THE ROW'S ACCOUNT OF IT IS NOT, AND THE FIGURE THAT CORRECTS IT WAS IN `MEASUREMENTS.md` THE WHOLE TIME.** CONFIRMED FIRST: the 6.21x is a genuine runtime difference and not an artifact of how M0-31 measured it. Its two halves differ in TWO ways — the node half IMPORTS `burn()` and calls it, the workerd half runs a hand-INLINED copy inside a `fetch` method — so a 2x2 was run to separate them (`bio-plane/test/m035-cpu-currency-probe.mjs`, quiet machine, three rungs, median of 5): **RUNTIME effect 6.37x / 6.41x with shape held; SHAPE effect 1.00x / 1.00x with runtime held.** Code shape contributes nothing and the 6.39x diagonal corroborates 6.21x to 3%. That hypothesis was declared before the run and the measurement killed it. **THEN THE ROW'S OWN BLOCKER TURNED OUT TO BE FALSE.** It says closing this takes `op=cpuprobe` on a DEPLOYED Worker, and M0-35's row carried that as a dependency on a DIST deploy. **No deploy was needed: FL-1 measured the deployed rate on 2026-08-08 and it has been in `MEASUREMENTS.md` at the `fl1-burn` row ever since — 40,000,000 reference iterations = 1,071.01 ms of BILLED CPU, from the Cloudflare GraphQL Analytics API, the platform's own observation from outside the isolate. That is 37,348 iter/ms deployed**, and the blocker was false for 39 days — *a blocker is a claim and nothing here audits one*. **SO THE GAP THIS ROW NAMES IS NOT THE BIG ONE.** node-vs-DEPLOYED is 1.35–2.02x; the 6.21x is node-vs-MINIFLARE, and **miniflare runs the reference loop 3.15–4.16x faster than the deployed runtime it stands in for.** The hazard was never that node and workerd disagree — it is that **miniflare is not the deployed runtime and is quoted as though it were.** **AND THE REMEDY THIS ROW PROPOSES WOULD NOT HAVE WORKED.** Stamping each figure with its runtime assumes the rate is a property of the runtime. Measured over five deterministic workloads shipped from ONE definition to both runtimes (`m035-workload-generality.mjs`, reproduced at 20M and 40M): **lcg 6.38x/6.40x, float 1.00x, typed 0.84x/0.83x, string 0.89x/0.82x, props 0.80x/0.84x — a spread of 7.8x, and the reference loop is the ONLY workload where workerd is faster.** The 6.4x is a property of the LCG loop, not of the runtime, **so the reference loop is one benchmark and not a currency: its rate transfers to no other work, and a runtime stamp would have made the record look settled while the conversion underneath stayed wrong.** **WHAT THE UNIT MAY BE USED FOR, which is the disposition rather than a mechanism:** a COUNT of reference iterations is runtime-portable and the 40M ceiling is sound in it — 14 COUNT sites owe no stamp at all. A RATE is meaningless without both its runtime AND its instance (four node readings span 18,496–27,586, a factor of 1.49, so a runtime stamp is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT). Converting any OTHER work through the loop is valid only when work and ceiling are denominated in the SAME runtime. **CENSUS** (`tools/m035-refiter-census.mjs`, 741 files walked, 4 generated excluded by construction including this item's own two): **9 RATE sites, which are restatements of only THREE distinct measurements** — the agreement of several documents being one source copied — **14 COUNT sites (10 live, 4 archived), 5 DERIVED groups hand-adjudicated** because no lexical matcher can see a figure spelled `257 ms`. **Three defects in the census's own matcher, recorded rather than smoothed: it scored the `35` out of this item's id as a figure, it counted its own prose, and a three-line attribution window stamped M-20's WORKERD figure `runtime=node` — an instrument built to catch mislabelled runtimes was mislabelling one.** All pinned in `--selftest`, 7/7. **THE TWO DERIVED FIGURES ARE NOT RE-TAKEN HERE AND ARE DELEGATED WITH THE MEASUREMENT ATTACHED — D-391.** CONTENT-PDF's OCR row is understated by 1.43x, NOT by the 6x this row predicts, and its verdict survives; M0-31's promote percentages are UNDETERMINED between ~20% and ~85% because it priced the ceiling at miniflare's rate (257 ms against a deployed 1,071 ms) while measuring the promote in miniflare too, so the two errors partly cancel by an amount nobody has bounded. Controls all as declared, including a noise-floor arm at 2.0% against a 6.4x effect and an arm that DID NOT ARM and failed loudly. `MEASUREMENTS.md` 2026-09-16 carries all of it. |

| D-379 | gap | 2026-09-15 | **THE THIRD READER OF A CAPTURE-AXIS GRADE IS A CACHE, AND AFTER REC-105 IT CARRIES THE OLD LETTER FORWARD BY A PATH NO MEMBER CHOSE.** Found by REC-105 while sweeping for a third reader that D-373's own pair-probe could not have seen, and DRIVEN rather than inferred (`bio-plane/test/rec105-reader-census.mjs`, run 2026-09-15 on the landing tree: the cache seeks `capture:B` for an inquiry whose walk now answers `C`). THE SHAPE. `bundles.inquiry_capture_strength` is REC-12's projection cache, written by `#writeStrengthProjection` from `strengthOf()` inside `op=promote`'s transaction and sought by `query.mjs`'s indexed `capture:` selector — so a member filtering *every question at B or better on the capture axis* is answered from it and not from the walk. **THE STALENESS IS NOT NEW AND IS DELIBERATE** — the method's own comment says *a cache and never the authority*, REC-12's suite proves the staleness on purpose, and `op=inquirystrength` exists precisely so anything that must be RIGHT calls `strengthOf()`. **WHAT IS NEW IS THE PATH INTO IT.** Until REC-105 the cache went stale when a leg BENEATH the inquiry was re-graded, which is a member's act on the inquiry's own subject matter. It can now also go stale because a DOCUMENT WAS RE-READ — a machine's act, on a different bundle, with no member involved — and the stale value is the STRONGER letter, which is the overclaiming direction. COSTS while open: a corpus-wide filter can return a question at a capture letter the walk no longer supports, with no sign at the filter; the row a member then opens tells them the truth, so the exposure is the LIST rather than the answer. and `plancheck` installing the copy on the SAME schedule as the hook and reporting it SEPARATELY, because an arming that half happened must not read as one that did. **EVERY WRITE TO THE SHARED MACHINERY IS AN ATOMIC RENAME, NOT A TRUNCATE-AND-WRITE** — `writeFileSync` truncates then fills, so a sibling's push landing inside that window execs a half-written shell script and is refused **in a session that changed nothing and could never attribute it**; that hazard existed in v1 and is closed here for the hook as well as the copy. **THE ROW'S OWN 6-OF-9 WAS RE-MEASURED AT THE ARTIFACT RATHER THAN INHERITED, and it had moved: 15 worktrees now, 10 PRESENT, 5 unguarded — the arithmetic is stale because branches rebased past M0-56, and the conclusion is intact because THE MAIN CHECKOUT IS STILL ONE OF THE FIVE.** A false-green risk was hunted and NOT found: a fallback is worthless if the old tree's `decided.mjs` treats `--check` as a free-text query (printing *no ruling mentions --check*, exit 0, certifying a push it never read); | D-428 | question | 2026-09-18 | **A CREATION AT A HIDDEN PROJECT'S ID, AND A PROJECT NAMED LIKE A HIDDEN ONE, BOTH SAY THE HIDDEN PROJECT EXISTS — AND THE SECOND SAYS WHICH ONE.** Found by REC-138 closing D-426, and deliberately NOT closed there because it is two rulings pulling against each other rather than a defect. (1) `op=promote` with `base: null` at an id that is taken answers `EXISTS`; at a never-minted id the same request CREATES the bundle, so no answer to the first can be byte-identical to the second — ids are one namespace, and project ids are client-chosen or drawn from `allocId`'s per-year sequence, so they are walkable by any member holding `create_projects`. **Inherent** while the id space is shared. (2) Membership v2 §7.1 makes a project's NAME unique across the instance (*"Uniqueness that a reader cannot see is not uniqueness"*), enforced at `promote` and `forkProject` by `NAME_TAKEN` — which answers the colliding project's `bundleId` AND its canonical `title`, to a caller who may not be able to see it. That collision is Bob's ruling (7.1) and it necessarily tells a creator that SOME project holds the name; handing back the other project's id and title is more than the ruling needs and is exactly what §7.9 forbids (*"not its existence, not its name"*). **Measured:** (1) is pinned as KNOWN in `project-sight.test.mjs` §6 (vera's creation at the hidden id answers `EXISTS`); (2) is read at the code, not driven. **COSTS WHILE OPEN:** an existence oracle for project ids to a member with `create_projects`, and a name-and-id oracle for any project whose title a member can guess. **CLOSING IT TAKES** a ruling first: whether §7.1's uniqueness yields to §7.9 for a caller who cannot see the other project (e.g. the collision is answered without naming the other project), and whether project ids should be minted by the plane rather than chosen, which would make (1) moot. Once ruled, (2) is one refusal's payload at two sites and one IC. — *PRIOR DISPOSITION, moved here verbatim at closing (LED-7 batch 1, 2026-09-19):* M8 · BOB's and RECORD's · **open, PARTLY CLOSED 2026-09-18 by REC-139 (IC-156, branch `worktree-agent-a590a3b1c261bf1ff`) on BOB #15's ruling at `7b733d07` (Membership v2 §7, *"What a refusal may say about a project the caller cannot see"*).** CLOSED: `NAME_TAKEN` names neither the other project's id nor its title, at `promote` and at `forkProject`, for every caller; and — the ruling's third point, found in the same class — an inquiry run's `projectGate.projects` counts only the citing projects its caller can see (it counted a hidden one). Driven in `bio-plane/test/project-disclosure.test.mjs`, eight control rows all as declared. **THE CREATION HALF — point (b) — CLOSED 2026-09-18 by REC-141 (IC-158, branch `worktree-agent-a12cdccbace704eb6`) on BOB #15's mechanism (Membership v2 §7, *"HOW the plane mints a project id"*):** a creation of a project, or any creation in the `PROJ-` namespace, that names an id is refused `PROJECT_ID_SUPPLIED` (C-59.1) before any id is looked up — one answer, taken or not, echoing no id; a fork's `newId` likewise (C-59.3); the plane mints the id in `allocId`'s pattern, writes it into the document's `id:` before hashing, refuses bytes already carrying one (C-59.2), and returns the id and the final sha. `project-sight.test.mjs` §6's KNOWN pin and `project-disclosure.test.mjs` §4's are CORRECTED; driven in `bio-plane/test/project-mint.test.mjs` (both row controls AS DECLARED). The Add surface and the fork form are UI-66's (DELEGATED in `CLAIMS.md`). A residual REC-141 names rather than closes: a project created before this under a NON-canonical id (not `PROJ-`-prefixed) still answers `EXISTS` to a creation of another type at that id; and the PROJ sequence number (as `op=allocid` already did) tells a creator how many project ids were minted before theirs. **STILL OPEN: (a) and (c); (b) is kept below as the record of what REC-141 closed.** (a) §7.1's instance-wide name uniqueness against §7.9 — BOB's, OPEN by his own ruling; provisionally uniqueness holds and the refusal reveals only the name the caller typed. (b, as it stood before REC-141) PLANE-MINTED PROJECT IDS, decided but NOT BUILT: the design does not say whether a caller-supplied id for a NEW project is REFUSED or IGNORED (nor whether `op=projectfork`'s `newId` is minted too, nor how the minted id reaches the document's own `id:` scalar), and REC-139's row said STOP on that — so the creation's `EXISTS` at a hidden id stands, pinned as KNOWN. A DESIGN GAP for Bob in §7; the build after it is RECORD's and touches every caller that chooses a project id today (`civicos-ui/app.html`'s Add surface mints `PROJ-<year>-<seq>-<slug>` in the browser through `op=allocid`, and most plane suites write literal ids). (c) NEW, raised by REC-139 and not decided: DEC-63's VERDICT is computed over every citing project, so a member is REFUSED a run over a question cited ONLY by projects they cannot see, and PERMITTED (as projectless) over one nobody cites — a one-bit signal that some unseen project draws on the question. Membership v2 §7 (BOB #15, at `7b733d07`) reads DEC-63 — Bob's own ruling of 2026-08-09 — as governing who may START and requiring no disclosure; whether the verdict itself is a §7.9 disclosure is not said there. | M8 · CLOSED 2026-09-19 IN FACT by LED-7 batch 1 (SCHEDULER): the NAME half by REC-139 (`32b81e09`, merged `3dcc70ed`; `NAME_TAKEN` names neither id nor title) on Bob's ruling that names stay unique (Membership v2 §7, `08826b77`); the CREATION half by REC-141 (`11aa7b13`; the plane mints opaque project ids and refuses a supplied one byte-identically). The residue — legacy caller-chosen ids — is a STATED LIMITATION in Membership v2 §7, "The legacy residue" (BOB #16), and REC-151 carries the other gated prefixes. | D-158 | defect | 2026-08-02 | **A signing key registered for a member who never ENROLLED reads `active` on `op=signerlist` and is refused by `op=ratify`.** MEASURED 2026-08-02 (session BOB, `MEASUREMENTS.md`, real `ssh-keygen` signatures through the real ratify path). `signerList()` (`store.mjs:5884`) reads the `signers` table alone; `gateFacts()` (`store.mjs:5920`) joins `members` and requires `s.status='active' AND m.status='active'`. The REVOCATION path agrees only because `memberSet` (`store.mjs:5857-5861`) cascades — it deletes the member's sessions and sets their signers to `revoked`. There is no cascade for the other direction: a member is `status='invited'` until enrolment (`store.mjs:5731`) and `signerAdd` checks only that the member EXISTS (`store.mjs:5874`), so an admin may register a key for someone who has never enrolled and `signerlist` reports it `active` while a signature from it returns `SIG_UNKNOWN_KEY`. **Not a blocker for RECONCILED §4 Q11**, which is settled YES on this measurement: the surface's *"you hold no active signing key"* is never a false alarm, because a key `signerlist` does not show active is one `ratify` will not accept. It is wrong only in the optimistic direction. **Two candidate fixes, and the second is preferable:** make `signerList()` join `members` so one predicate serves both, or make `signerAdd` refuse a member who has not enrolled — the join is the smaller change but leaves a row that means nothing; refusing at write keeps the table honest and is the shape `signerAdd`'s existing NO_SUCH_MEMBER check already reaches for. Whichever ships, the other view must be asserted against it. | M10 · CLOSED 2026-09-19 AS A DEBT ROW by LED-7 batch 1 (SCHEDULER) — PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-158, at the top of the backlog: verified NOT YET FIXED at the code (`Store.signerAdd` checks only that the member exists and writes `status='active'`); the row's own preferred fix is the task. *Prior disposition, kept as the record:* M10 - open · REC-15/UI-17 pre-flight; scoped in the 2026-08-02 BOB INBOX entry |

| D-155 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A project declares the evidentiary strength its work requires, and the published case carries that declaration beside the strength it reached.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-17). `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §12 has recorded since v0.1 that a claim *"needs a standard of proof attached and that is doctrine rather than architecture — the next design conversation"*; this is that conversation. The question was what makes an unsupported case harder to state, and both options this project generated put the standard in the wrong place: a global floor would refuse honest weak findings, and no floor leaves nothing to fall short of. **The standard belongs to the GROUP and to a particular piece of their work** — a project convened to refer something to an auditor needs a different bar from one convened to decide whether a thing is worth looking at, and both are legitimate, so a single system-wide answer was always going to be wrong for one of them. **This is the PROJECT axis and it is not the AUDIENCE axis**, which is why it does not contradict `research/AUDIENCES.md` §5's ruling that per-audience relaxation is *"a structural prior by role — the same defect as a suspicion flag, with the sign reversed"* and that a threshold belongs on a rendering and never on ratification. A project-declared bar is authored by the group, before the work, about its own intentions; nobody's standard is set by who they are. That file is not edited (see D-153's reasoning); its ruling stands and is about a different axis. SHAPE: `project` frontmatter gains `required_strength{capture, connection}` — **a PAIR per R2**, because a scalar would re-collapse the two axes in the one field a reader is most likely to quote. C-2.9 already requires a non-empty `objective` on the same object, so the check has a precedent beside it. `op=publishpreflight` refuses `BELOW_PROJECT_STRENGTH` naming the axis; the escape is amending the project's declared bar, which is an authored, dated, on-the-record act visible in the published case — you can lower your own bar, you cannot do it quietly. A project with no declared strength gates nothing and the case SAYS so rather than rendering a blank, since an absent bar is not a bar of zero. An inquiry outside any project has no bar and inherits none. | M9 · CLOSED 2026-09-19 IN FACT by LED-7 batch 4 (SCHEDULER #2), verified at the code: DEC-17 is ENACTED and the published case carries BOTH halves this row asked for. `store.mjs` writes a `required_strength:` block into the case frontmatter (`declared`, `source`, `project`, `capture`, `connection`, `detail`, at :7862), and check **C-41.12** asserts it at the CASE level with “an ABSENT bar is STATED as absent rather than shown as blank” carried word for word, plus the per-axis grade check beneath it — which is the strength REACHED beside the strength REQUIRED. Note the bar MOVED after this row was written: DEC-72 clause 2 makes it a property of the PROJECT told to the publishing act, so it is the case's and no longer any member's (CASE-5b). The row's requirement is met at the new site, not the one it named. *Prior disposition, kept as the record:* M10 - open · REC-14, REC-15, UI-18 |

| D-359 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **NO FORMAT ENTRY EMITS A SHEET'S ROW AND COLUMN EXTENT OR A SLIDE'S SHAPE COUNT, SO THE INNER HALF OF TWO OF THE THREE OFFICE ARMS' OUT-OF-RANGE REFUSAL STILL HAS NOTHING TO READ.** D-354's residue, found by CAP-12 while closing it, and MEASURED against all six entries' returns rather than assumed: `xlsxText`/`odsText` return `sheets: [{sheet, name, hidden, text, undetermined}]` — names and no dimensions; `pptxText`/`odpText` return `slides: [{slide, ref, part, hidden, text}]` — no shape count; `docxText`/`odtText` return `paragraphs[]`, whose LENGTH is the count, which is why the paragraph arm is fully fed and the other two are not. CPDF-14 keeps the two apart by naming its columns differently (`agree w/ floor`, `digits DIVERGING from the floor`) and never mixing them, which is the right handling of the gap and not a substitute for closing it. WHAT IT COSTS: every future engine measurement — CPDF-10's eventual external tier, any CPDF-13 calibration re-probe, any tesseract fleet-member score — is either n=1 on accuracy or n>1 on agreement, and a GO/NO-GO that needs both at once cannot be had. The partition is printable, so the arm must PRINT both halves per suite and floor the fed half, or it becomes the blind walk REC-70 records. Until it lands, a real-plane suite asserting a refusal by name may not name a code the surface cannot reach. — *PRIOR DISPOSITION, MOVED HERE VERBATIM AT CLOSING (SCHEDULER #2, 2026-09-19):* M8 · open — owner UI | M8 · CLOSED 2026-09-19 AS A DEBT ROW by SCHEDULER #2 — PLACED as a BACKLOG task under its own id, D-433, with D-414 in the INSTRUMENT CLUSTER, of which it is the fourth member: M0-78 (arms that do not arm, and a census blind to that), D-414 (a walk crediting a generator's body to the method above it), D-355 (two drivers red on a green `main`, still in DEBT) and this. Placed with them so a slot-filling pass cannot separate them; the fix is the row's own and needs no design. |
